6066a5b85a0c6d1f6397914b8666b0fd0e5fd7eb:vendor/bun/Cargo.toml:132:# `panic = "abort"` — the `std::panic` hook in `bun_crash_handler` aborts,
6066a5b85a0c6d1f6397914b8666b0fd0e5fd7eb:vendor/bun/Cargo.toml:144:# requires the *linker's* LLVM to be ≥ rustc's bundled LLVM, so the build
6066a5b85a0c6d1f6397914b8666b0fd0e5fd7eb:vendor/bun/Cargo.toml:157:# (minutes) is in the way. `bun run build:release --cargo-profile=release-dev`.
6066a5b85a0c6d1f6397914b8666b0fd0e5fd7eb:vendor/bun/Cargo.toml:170:# `bun_shim_impl.exe` — the Windows .bin/ launcher PE that gets
6066a5b85a0c6d1f6397914b8666b0fd0e5fd7eb:vendor/bun/Cargo.toml:171:# `include_bytes!`'d into bun.exe and copied next to every package binary by
6066a5b85a0c6d1f6397914b8666b0fd0e5fd7eb:vendor/bun/Cargo.toml:172:# `bun install`. Zig built it freestanding ReleaseFast (no libc,
6066a5b85a0c6d1f6397914b8666b0fd0e5fd7eb:vendor/bun/Cargo.toml:180:# release regardless of bun's own profile: a debug bun should still write

6066a5b85a0c6d1f6397914b8666b0fd0e5fd7eb:vendor/bun/LICENSE.md:5:Bun statically links JavaScriptCore (and WebKit) which is LGPL-2 licensed. WebCore files from WebKit are also licensed under LGPL2. Per LGPL2:
6066a5b85a0c6d1f6397914b8666b0fd0e5fd7eb:vendor/bun/LICENSE.md:7:> (1) If you statically link against an LGPL’d library, you must also provide your application in an object (not necessarily source) format, so that a user has the opportunity to modify the library and relink the application.
6066a5b85a0c6d1f6397914b8666b0fd0e5fd7eb:vendor/bun/LICENSE.md:9:You can find the patched version of WebKit used by Bun here: <https://github.com/oven-sh/webkit>. If you would like to relink Bun with changes:
6066a5b85a0c6d1f6397914b8666b0fd0e5fd7eb:vendor/bun/LICENSE.md:15:This compiles JavaScriptCore, compiles Bun’s `.cpp` bindings for JavaScriptCore (which are the object files using JavaScriptCore) and outputs a new `bun` binary with your changes.

## Release and extracted-smoke topology

Mechanical Rust SDK/static, loaded-native, and existing libbun-backend migration follows only after that SCC closes and consumes the compiler-owned declaration-contract member; it does not schedule a general TypeScript SDK. TLS, string faults, empty/default settlement, DTO/parts/raw `ImportId`, precommit `Arc`/clone/`OnceLock`, family/site selectors, legacy `defineContract`/normalized-JSON authority, and second commits are forbidden.
